### Design-system build — spec 082

Expand → migrate → contract. 084 expands (tokens; zero `.tsx` churn), 085–088 migrate the consumers,
089 contracts (the gates are green only when the sweep is complete).
**084 done** — the tokens are in and the contrast gate runs on every `npm run lint`; the app now
renders steel-blue neutrals against warm status badges, the expected intermediate state.
**085 done** — every grid now paints from the tokens out of one params block, so the grid can no
longer diverge from `global.css`.
**086 done** — the badge idiom is one component taking a severity; the two feature tone maps (and
the duplicate third) are gone, and `go` found its first consumer in the BBY validity marker.
**087 done** — al-dawaa gold/navy now live only inside the mark: the login Editorial Split swaps its
ground to `--brand-panel` (kickers lose their gold, marks stay gold), the home hero becomes a plain
`--card` tool (watermark + gold kicker dropped), both `text-[#FDC801]` classes and the four brand
`text-white` sites are gone, and the dead `auth.json` `subtitle` key is deleted. `-\[#` is zero across
`src/`.
**089 done — the design-system build is closed.** `npm run lint` runs three gates; the third,
`tools/check-palette.mjs`, fails on a raw palette class, an arbitrary colour value, a `white`/`black`
utility or a bare hex anywhere in `src/` outside `global.css` and the mark. The ticket's open question
was resolved as it recommended: the hex pattern is widened past D-12's `-\[#` to `#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}`,
so a colour hidden in a `.ts` string — the one place this app ever hid one — is caught by lint rather
than by review. The four deliberate literals (three `bg-black/50` scrims, the login QR quiet zone)
are file-scoped exclusions carrying their reason inline.

### Document Details build — spec 083

Region by region, each slice taking one region of Screen 2 the whole way down (payload → pure rule →
component → i18n → test). **090 is slice 0**: it bootstraps vitest against the five captured payloads
and cuts the pill rail, retiring the spec's biggest unknown — that rules derived from real data hold
against that data. 093 needs only the runner, so it runs in parallel with 091/092. The action bar is
094 rather than earlier because it is the only region whose change deletes state elsewhere in the
page (`pendingNote`). 095 lands the RTL respellings byte-identical under LTR; 096 is the acceptance
drive.
**096 done — the Document Details rework is closed, and with it map 068.** The rebuilt screen drives
end to end (39/39) with the six region drives still green beside it, and the both-theme pass across
all five captured documents found no arrangement defect. Its one recorded finding is a *design-system*
question, not a screen one: `--brand-panel` is fixed in both themes, so the identity band separates
from the page at 13.54:1 in light and 1.18:1 in dark.

## OMS access gate + the empty-menu state

The one nav group with no probe (`menu-model.ts:23-29` names deliveries as *the* "always visible"
example) turns out to be an open **write** door: every `SdDocument/*` endpoint carries
`ApiKeyEndpointFilter` and no grant filter, so any authenticated session — including a store employee who
has just activated and holds no role — can reschedule or update a delivery document. Closing it makes the
empty menu common, so the empty state ships first. Server halves are BackOffice
[749](C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.issues\749-oms-web-screen-grant-seed.md) (grant seed) and
[750](C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.issues\750-sddocument-web-door.md) (the cookie-only `SdDocumentWeb/*` door).

- [124](124-no-visible-menu-empty-state.md) — A signed-in user with nothing to open is told so, not shown a blank page · **done** · blocked by: — · frontend-only, no backend dependency, ships first · the `noAccess` card in the `home` namespace (three keys: title / diagnosis / action — "contact IT support", never "your administrator") plus the part that is not cosmetic: `useVisibleMenu` grows a `settled` flag, because it hides a gated item while its probe is *pending* and the moment 125 gates the last ungated group the menu is empty on **first paint for everyone** — a naive `length === 0` check would flash the card on every load. Two call sites, so the return type changes rather than adding a parallel hook that can drift. Root cause it explains: `UpsertEmployeeAsync` writes `UaEmployee` only; the `UaUser` shell is minted by **first role assignment in Authz Admin**, and until then `AuthorizationSnapshotLoader` answers every probe with an empty list, silently
- [125](125-oms-behind-the-access-probe.md) — OMS goes behind an access probe, and its write doors stop being open · **code-complete** (client built, drive 28/28 on a stubbed wire; runtime-blocked on 750 being deployed + the server-refuses-a-reschedule check) · blocked by: 124 ✔, BackOffice 750 (**deployed**, not merely merged) · repoint `features/oms/*` to `SdDocumentWeb/*`, fold the bare `searchDeliveries` into a `deliveriesApi` object with `access()`, `accessProbe` on the leaf keyed `['oms','access']` (same key as the page guard ⇒ one call), and **two** guards — `DeliveriesPage` on `canOpenList`, `DocumentDetailsPage` on `canOpenDetail`. The second is the easy one to skip and the one that matters: `/oms/document/:no` is deep-linkable, `router.tsx` carries no per-route permission metadata, and a list-only guard leaves the write screen reachable to anyone who knows a document number. ⚠ this probe **fails closed** — no 404-tolerant catch like `Notifications/Access`/`Bby/Access`, because the endpoint ships with 750 and the thing behind it is a write door, which is exactly why it cannot merge before 750 is deployed. OQ1 is the likeliest way it goes wrong: the current OMS team must be *bound to the role* first, or the gate locks out the people it admits. Built: the probe landed in `@/core/oms/api` (`OMS_ACCESS_KEY` + `omsAccessApi.access()`) rather than on `deliveriesApi` — both OMS pages guard on it and a feature may not import a feature, the ticket-118 move. Two fixes beyond the letter: the guards carry the menu's `staleTime: Infinity` too (a mismatched one refetched on mount and could empty the OMS group under a working screen), and a FAILED probe now says the check is unavailable instead of telling an entitled operator to ask for a grant they hold. Eight sibling document/grid drives were repointed to the new door and re-run green

## The call center goes to the web (map 126)

Phase 1 replaces the WPF call center (CC1 `NewOrderController` + CC2) for the **plain CLCN cash
order only**, on an **engine-as-a-service** substrate: SIS.Api opens a real server-side
`CallCenterOrder` `PosTransaction` (it already ships `SIS.Pos 26.4.113`), resumed per request off
the persisted snapshot, and the client sends **intent, never money**. Backend halves are minted as
BackOffice issues (`C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.issues\`) — the engine and SIS.Api ship to the tills
too. Stands on BackOffice map [244](C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.issues\244-newpos-callcenter-order-entry.md)
+ spec [267](C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.issues\267-cc-order-entry-submission-spec.md), which already
shipped the doctype, the once-only `CallCenter/SubmitOrder` mint, and the submit orchestration.

### Owner gap review, 2026-07-27 — six features the charting missed (154–159)

Raised on reviewing 138's prototype: delivery type · payment type · delivery fees · stock in other
stores · price check · redeem coupon · add new loyalty. They split three ways — **already in scope
but never drawn** (159), **missing from the frozen contract** (154, 155), and **never charted at all**
(156's waiver half, 157, 158). Two owner rulings taken at review time: the delivery fee is
**rule-driven with no manual waiver**, and price check + stock-in-other-stores are **both phase 1**.

- [154](154-fulfilment-mode-and-store-choice.md) — Fulfilment mode, and how the store gets chosen when nothing derives it · **done 2026-07-27** · map 126 · blocked by: — · grilling · **HITL (7 rulings taken)** · **both modes in phase 1** — owner: "these are the bare minimum". Contract → **v1.1, additive** ([§2.2](assets/136-cc-contract/CONTRACT.md) + new fixture [09](assets/136-cc-contract/09-fulfilment-flip.json) + dated §10 entry); server half BackOffice [826](C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.issues\826-cc-fulfilment-mode.md). **Three facts dissolved most of the ticket**: 🚩 CC2 holds **no line entry at all** (the header is captured, then POS rings the items), so "can the mode change mid-basket?" has **no WPF precedent** — the web *creates* the question by making the engine session live from the first keystroke; 🚩 the gate the ticket cited (`CanEditOrderType`, `IsFulfillmentEnabled`) is **edit-mode, not basket state**, so it argues for no lines-exist gate; 🚩 `open` **already** seeds the plant from `entryStore` with `canAddItem: true` (fixture 01), so map note 6 holds under either mode and pickup needs **no new opening move** — 135's caret-on-phone ruling is untouched. Ruled: flip allowed whenever `status: open`; **the flip never moves the plant** (which is what keeps this additive — pickup→delivery re-derives through `setAddress`'s existing path, so 129's door is reached the way it already was); pickup store = **whole estate unfiltered** via existing `setStore` over off-door `StoreDetails`, **134's no-store-dimension ruling stands** (129's atomic refusal is the material constraint); address **hidden and retained** (shown-but-optional rejected — the builder discards it, so the agent would watch keyed data vanish), slot hidden, both dropping from `submitBlockers`. **1.1 not 2.0**: `address` was already nullable, §5.1 already defined `setStore` as an operator override, `submitBlockers` was already a varying list — a v1.0 client renders a pickup order wrongly but never *mis-renders money*, the hazard the hard stop exists for. 🚩 **Corrects [156](156-delivery-fee-shared-rule.md)**: the threshold is **not** missing and the contract invented nothing — `POSController.NewPos.cs:8951` computes the whole CallCenterOrder fee rule and already keys on the mode; `thresholdGross` quotes `POSCommon.ShippingMinimumAmount`. Two open items recorded not invented: a pickup order has **no collection time**, and `plantSource` now does double duty

## The call center goes to the web — build, phase 2 (spec 180)

Tracer tickets sliced from spec 180 — everything the console owes contract **v1.1 → v1.10**, after
spec 160's frozen-v1.0 build (161–174) shipped and its seven carve-outs were all resolved. 🚩 The
slicing re-verified the spec's load-bearing claim and **corrected it**: `ConsoleShell` really does
already accept all seven handlers and `header-chips.ts` really does emit all seven chips with
`CallCenterConsolePage` supplying none — but the spec said *two* surfaces are undrawn and there are
**three**. `AddressPicker` is **pick-only**: no create form, no edit form, no district picker, because
179 was a decision session and not a prototype. Model gap is small (`PriceCheckResult`,
`StockElsewhereResult`, `canPriceCheck`), so no prefactor — each slice adds its own. Four chains:
**{181 → 191}** (repair, then the keyboard), **{182 → 184}**, **{185 → 186}**, **{187 → 188}**, with
183, 189, 190 free-standing and **192 last** behind five of them.

Two routes on `api.ts`, neither a session verb (no `transactionId`, no busy retry, one `requestId` each). - [191](191-arrow-then-enter-adds-the-highlighted-row.md) — arrowThenEnterAddsTheHighlightedRow · **done** · blocked by: 181 · 🚩 **fixes the map's headline feature being mouse-only** — neither a search row's *Add* nor a guidance card's had **any** keyboard path — and it is the **only slice in the spec with no server dependency at all**, so it is unaffected if 804/879 slip. 153 killed the obvious design first: a Gmail-style *armed when you are not typing* grammar cannot work here because the resting focus is a text box **twice over** (the rail `autoFocus`es the phone field; the search box **re-focuses itself after every landed add**, deliberately) — so the grammar works **from inside the box the agent is already in**. `↓`/`↑` move a highlight; **nothing is highlighted until the first press**, because 131's non-sargable `LIKE '%…%'` makes the top row a relevance **guess** and a one-key add of a guess puts a line on a live order — two keys is the price of every add being aimed. Highlight resets per term, inert while the gate is shut or an add is in flight, and `Enter` calls **the same handler the button calls** — never a second predicate. Built as a module because 192 reuses it · **built**: `highlight.ts` carries the highlight **with the term it was set against**, so a new question drops it by construction, and a shorter answer to the SAME question **clamps** to the last row rather than losing the agent’s aim; it holds no row, no item number and no verb, which is what lets 192 reuse it over the palette. 🚩 Ruled: `↑` from nothing highlights **nothing** — wrapping to the last row would arm `Enter` on the least relevant guess. The gate in `ItemSearchPanel` is ONE expression (`add.onAdd !== null && add.pending === null`) — the same condition the row’s *Add* is drawn and disabled on — and `Enter` calls the same handler with the same two arguments the button passes. A key still finishing a word is ignored (an IME’s own `Enter`/arrows reach the handler as ordinary keys, and half this console searches in Arabic). `GuidanceStrip` deliberately UNCHANGED: 153 gives the card’s *Add* its keyboard path through 192’s palette rows, and a second highlight armed from the same box would be two lists competing for one `Enter`. The aim is **announced, not only painted** — 153’s build note binds it to `aria-activedescendant` over the rows (combobox/listbox/option) and never to a focus move, since the caret must stay in the box. Proof: 13 pure + drive boxes 41–43 (the negative taken first, with the gate wide open; the ARIA aim asserted both ways — it names the row, and names nothing while nothing is highlighted)
- [192](192-ctrl-k-reaches-every-order-act.md) — ctrlKReachesEveryOrderActWithoutEndingTheCall · **done** · blocked by: 182, 183, 187, 189, 191 · **lands last** — a palette row cannot exist for a verb that is not wired, and its five blockers are the five slices that supply its rows. `Ctrl+K` opens from anywhere **including inside any text box** (must `preventDefault()`; it is Chrome's omnibox key and IS interceptable) and is **inert while any `<dialog>` is open** — a palette over a confirmation sheet is two truths on one screen. Built on `core/ui/Modal`'s native `<dialog>`, so trap and restore are free. Rows: **actionable offers first**, read from the strip's own view model so the palette and the top-bar count **cannot disagree** (`Alt+1..3` rejected — cards re-order, so a number is a position not an offer), then the order verbs, then the two terminals. 🚩 **Nothing on the keyboard can end a call**: submit/abandon are palette rows only, sorted **last** and **never auto-highlighted**, so a query matching only `Abandon call` highlights nothing and the agent must press `↓`. 🚩 **A refused verb is a disabled row carrying its reason** — the console's **one deliberate exception** to *a control the door would refuse is worse than no control*, because the law is about a control the hand lands on and the palette is a question the agent **asked**; disabled rows stay highlightable and `Enter` does nothing, since skipping them would hide the very reason the exception exists. The palette is the whole cheat sheet — no `?` (it types into the box the agent is in), no `F1`. Line verbs stay out: the palette is one level deep and its object is the **order**, which keeps *void* aimed at a line the agent is looking at. No server dep · ⚠ name the drive `command-palette-drive.mjs` — `tools/palette-drive.mjs` is taken by the POS colour palette · **built**: `palette-model.ts` takes the strip's `GuidanceView` (read ONCE in `ConsoleShell`, beside the top-bar count, so drift is impossible by construction) and a record of **handlers** — never `capabilities` booleans: a row is enabled exactly when the page handed it the same handler the chip, the rail or the receipt already reads, so an act withdrawn from one surface is withdrawn from both, and `ConsoleShell` needed **no new prop**. 🚩 An **offer row hands off to the item search** (172's `onSearchRest`) rather than adding — a near-miss names no material, so an add row would need `ResolvePrereq` run for every offer the instant the palette opens; the strip resolves one card on demand and the palette gives it the keyboard path. 🚩 The aim is carried against the query **AND the row ids** (`paletteQuestion`): the rows are rebuilt from the live `SessionState`, so an add landing under an open palette would otherwise slide a carried index — which `highlight.ts` clamps to the LAST row, *Abandon call*. 🚩 `Ctrl+K` is `preventDefault()`ed **before** the inert check — *inert* must mean this console does nothing, not that Chrome takes the omnibox. Refusals are worded from the chip row's OWN `fulfilment.locked.*` / `payment.locked.*` families and a dead submit borrows the receipt's `submitBlockers`; only §6.3's attach-before-address ordering is quoted outright, guarded on `hasCaller` so it can never be a wrong refusal. ⚠ **Done-when vs 153, resolved to 153**: submit has no modal anywhere on this console, so *sorted last* + *never auto-aimed* are the guards (both asserted) and a **deliberate** ↓ then Enter places the order exactly as the receipt's button does — asserted as ONE `Submit` with one `requestId`; gating submit behind a confirmation is a new ticket about the receipt. Proof: 29 pure + `command-palette-drive.mjs` 44/44

## The call center goes to the web — the caller's open request (spec 193)

Phase 3 of the client track of map 126, from owner grilling 2026-07-29/30. A pharmacist's **sales
request** (category `'Q'` / SREQ) becomes a web-placed order: WPF has done this since
`NewOrderController.RequestLookup`, and on the web the `RefDocumentNo` / `DocumentReason` fields exist
all the way down to `AddSdDocumentHeader` with **nothing filling them** — so a web order can never
convert a request and the TMRA picking fee the OMS charges off the reason code never gets charged.
Server half is BackOffice **880** (contract v1.11).

- [193](193-linked-sales-request-spec.md) — The caller's open request becomes this order · **ready** · spec — consumable by /to-tickets · 🚩 The owner's opening proposal — **lock the customer for the life of the order**, change means abandon — is deliberately **not** in the spec: `removeCustomer` clearing the link closes the same vector (link caller A's request, swap to B, submit, and the 055b spine completes A's request against B's order) while 871 §6's ruling stands that "the ordinary *wrong caller, same items* correction must not cost the basket". **Linkable only on an empty basket** is the restriction the rest of the design hangs off — it kills the plant re-price, the copied-meets-typed merge and every quantity ambiguity at once — and it is what forces **unlink to be a full undo** rather than WPF's stamp-drop, since a basket left un-emptied could never be re-linked. Store + items copy for every reason; **TMRA additionally forces pickup and paid-online**, because the caller is standing in the pharmacy and Tamara settles online. 🚩 The picker deliberately does **not** reuse `DocumentDetailsPage` (no view-only mode — WPF had `ViewOnly = true`; it would put change-store/close-request under an agent's hand mid-call — and `callcenter → oms` is a boundary violation): the console needs the lines anyway to copy them, so the picker shows what it is about to copy. Scoped by **loyalty id only** — one household number is one household, and phone scoping surfaces a relative's request · dep: BackOffice **880**
- [194](194-the-callers-open-request-becomes-the-order.md) — theCallersOpenRequestBecomesThisOrder · **done 2026-07-30** · blocked by: — · ⚠ **wholly undrawn** — no picker, no chip, no model, and the server routes are being built alongside, so this is a build against a contract rather than a wiring. **The count is drawn, the modal is not**: attaching a caller with open requests puts a count in the rail (an unnoticed request is the failure the spec exists to prevent) but nothing opens by itself, because the agent is mid-greeting and a picker over the basket takes the call away from them. **One press, one act** — `linkRequest` carries one `requestId` and the console must **not** loop `addItem`, which would be N ids for one agent action. 🚩 The interesting half is the **skipped-line report**: lines the server refused or found below ATP were **not** added, and a below-ATP line may never arrive already-added because `HasBelowAtp` is a fraud signal and a flag nobody saw proves nothing. The linked card holds **no money** (the request is unpriced) · dep: BackOffice **880** · **built 2026-07-30** — 32 pure + drive **61/61**, 783 suite-wide, lint and build green. 🚩 **The offer reads `canLinkRequest` and inspects the basket ONLY where that capability is absent** (a pre-v1.11 server), so *linkable ⇔ empty basket* has one home — the server's — and the count is also silent once `header.linkedRequest` is set, because the card replaces it. The reason renders as its description or as **nothing**: there is no `?? reason` fallback anywhere and `TMRA` is asserted absent from the picker's whole text. 🚩 Review moved real code twice: the skipped row was classified on the figures' mere presence AND had its code dropped, so a refusal that happened to ship stock numbers would have been drawn as an *add anyway* with the server's own reason erased — it is now classified on a real **shortfall** (`requested > available`, `belowAtpAsk`'s own rule, since the handle leads to that very sheet) and the code rides on **every** row; and a landed add now **prunes its row** (`reportWithout`, on ANY add of that item, the last row taking the banner with it), because *this did not land* over a basket that now holds the line is the console arguing with the receipt. The report is the console's THIRD in-the-flow banner rather than picker furniture — *add anyway* opens the below-availability `<dialog>` and two open dialogs are two truths on one screen; `ConsoleBanner` lost its tone-derived dismiss selector in the process (three banners, two tones). Open question settled **under the caller card**: a request belongs to the CALLER, arrives with them and leaves with them. The picker's shut-gate wording is a **guard, not the normal path** (the count never offers a picker onto a shut gate), so the two unreachable reasons were cut from the i18n rather than left as plausible dead copy. **No re-read on the picker's open** — 195 forbids pre-checking the request, and the guard is the submit refusal. `CONTEXT.md` gained **Sales request** (and why it is not a *request close*), **Linked request**, **Skipped line**. Unlink and `REQUEST_ALREADY_CONVERTED` are 195's · ⚠ dep BackOffice **880 UNBUILT** — both routes and all of v1.11 stubbed to §3/§4's documented shape, said in the drive's own header; against today's server the read 404s and the rail is silent, which is the same silence a caller with no requests gets
- [195](195-unlinking-and-the-request-that-went-away.md) — unlinkingIsAFullUndoAndTheRequestThatWentAwayIsNamed · **done 2026-07-30** · blocked by: 194 · split out because these two rules are sharp and carry the real risk. **Unlink removes the copied lines and re-shuts the store gate**, so it asks first and its confirm says *the caller stays attached* — the two acts sit next to each other in the rail. `removeCustomer` clearing the link is a **server** change with a **client obligation to stay quiet**, so the client's correctness is an absence and the drive proves it by reading the whole screen for the request number rather than checking one element. 🚩 `REQUEST_ALREADY_CONVERTED` is reachable in normal operation — another agent or the till can convert the request mid-call, and 055c then refuses the order; without the mapping it arrives as the transient `SUBMIT_UNAVAILABLE` and the agent retries forever on an order that will never post. ⚠ **No pre-submit re-check**: it would narrow the window and invite a reader to believe it was closed · dep: BackOffice **880** · **built 2026-07-30** — 9 new pure cases (41 in `linked-request.test.ts`, 792 suite-wide) + drive **92/92**; typecheck, lint and build green. 🚩 **BackOffice 880 was BUILT mid-ticket** and capture 15 (`136-cc-contract/15-linked-sales-request.json`, v1.11) landed with the whole scenario on the wire — which caught 194 calling the wrong door (`CustomerRequests` needs `?transactionId=`, the session row being what holds the loyalty id the scope comes from; fixed here, and still no customer id on the wire) and pinned the unlink's own bytes: `linkedRequest: null`, `lines: []`, `plantSource: seededAtOpen`, `canAddItem` **false** with `STORE_NOT_CHOSEN` back, `canLinkRequest` **true** again, caller untouched. One surprise from the capture: the prefilled `sourceReference` **survives** both the unlink and the caller's removal — it is the ORDER's field from §4.4 onward, so the drive's whole-screen sweep exempts the chip row rather than the console scrubbing the header behind the server's back. `unlinkCost(state)` takes the state and NOTHING else, re-derived each render, so a link that landed four of six offers to remove four and a link that vanishes under the open sheet closes it; 🚩 review moved real code once — the confirm read its store off the REQUEST's stamp, which 880 (*what shipped* §3, a request naming no store copies no store) would have drawn as a blank claiming a gate re-shut that never opened, and which spec 193's *the address still wins the plant* would have made name a branch the order had left; it now reads `header.plant` + `plantSource`, silent where there was no chosen store to lose; `keepsCustomer: true` is a FIELD, like `SubmitFailure.orderOpen`, because the sheet must not be trusted to remember the one fact that makes the control pressable. `REQUEST_ALREADY_CONVERTED` needed no new failure kind — `readSubmitFailure` already answers it `retryable: false`, asserted from both directions since the two SWAPPING is the defect — so 195 adds only the naming and a closed set of one escape, silent where the order holds no link, and it **drops** the server's sentence (which names the same request) on 189's `COUPON_REVERSAL_REFUSED` precedent. The sheet is `ConfirmSheet` + an `UnlinkConfirm` body (a third body, not a third mechanism), no `reissue` because there is no token. ⚠ Named not done: the palette (192) still lists neither *view requests* nor *unlink* — `VERB_ORDER` predates the request surface, and both belong to whoever revisits it

"The page holds" needed no code (the page is a field of the query; a mutation only invalidates) and is asserted so nobody breaks it. Excel guards: `="…"` on employeeId/phone (quoting alone loses a leading zero), `'` on formula-leading free text, BOM + `sep=,` + CRLF. `null` last-login is an **empty cell, not "Never"** (a word kills date sorting). Built: pure `csv.ts` (writer + filename, takes a **label resolver** so its suite drives it with the real locale file — a missing key fails as a raw key in a cell) + `export.ts` (walk + download). - [151](151-ua-users-export-at-scale.md) — exportingEverybodyWarnsFirstAndCancelsWithNoPartialFile · **done 2026-07-27** · blocked by: 150 · everything exportable incl. `all`, but >500 rows raises a confirm naming rows + wait (~45 s for ~6,000); `phoneGap` ~400 lands just under deliberately. Cancellable sonner progress (one toast id re-fired per page, cancel flips a **ref** so the running walk sees it without a render), walk never touches the mounted query's cache. 🚩 **The governing rule both ways: cancel or any `ApiError` ⇒ no file at all** — the walk **throws** rather than returning what it had, which is that rule expressed where it can be tested. Built: `needsConfirm` + `estimateWalkSeconds` as pure functions (the threshold is one testable fact, not a `>` on a screen) and two named errors, `ExportCancelledError` + `ExportRunawayError` — the runaway needs its own because `apiErrorMessage` would render it as a generic "unexpected", and "the walk never ended" is different news from "the server refused". 🚩 Review caught a real hole: the confirm read `list.data?.totalMatches ?? 0`, so an export fired before the first read settled saw **0 matches**, skipped the dialog and walked 6,000 people with no progress and no Cancel — the button is now dead until a count lands. `exporting` is taken **before** the confirm too (the dialog is awaited; a live button could start a second walk). Drive now 70/70, its new sections delaying the stub's `all` pages so a mid-walk cancel is reachable, and letting one run finish to prove 6,000 identities land exactly once. ⚠ the >500 confirm is a wait-time device, **not** a control — never write it up as one

Disabled neither moves nor is renamed. Track is now **one column per card** (`repeat(var(--card-count),minmax(0,1fr))`), and the drive measures one row of equal widths at 1600px *and* 1024px, in both arrangements. The drive runs its whole length on the SIX-card envelope and flips the field on at the end with a reload — which is how the card reaches production when 805 deploys, with no client release. Drive 85/85. **dep: BackOffice 805**. 🚩 Do NOT add a cards-sum check — they already don't sum (three universes), and this card overlaps Disabled on purpose (a leaver who activated still counts)

## Nphies to web — map 196

Charting the port of the WPF Nphies module (`C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\Sartawi.POS\Nphies\`) to
oms-react. Destination: **a sized estimate + a `/to-spec`-ready spec**. **MAP CLOSED 2026-08-01 —
both halves exist: the estimate is 204 (35–46 developer-days, two teams) and the spec is
[209](209-nphies-web-spec.md), `status: ready`. Twelve tickets, no fog left. Next step is
`/to-tickets` against 209.** The Nphies service's **own source** is on disk at
`C:\Work\DMSCO\nphies\Service\NphiesService` — read it before charting anything further.
Four premise-changing finds: the Nphies
integration already lives server-side, **the Nphies service's own source is on disk** at
`C:\Work\DMSCO\nphies\Service\`, **v1 is one claim type with no mode selectors and no hand-typed
identity** (199) — deliberately the smallest thing the team can react to — and, latest and largest,
**an authorization is an engine document on the Till Submission Platform, not a JSON post** (208),
so the web screen is a live engine session and the map's cheapest term grows a session surface.

- [196](196-nphies-to-web-map.md) — Nphies to web · **done 2026-08-01** · map · **destination reached: estimate [204](204-nphies-the-estimate.md) + spec [209](209-nphies-web-spec.md)** · Destination: sized estimate backed by a ready spec. Key fact from charting: `NphiesService.cs` is pure `HttpClient` against a standalone REST service at `172.23.27.40:8065` — **this is a UI port, not an integration build**. Counterweight: `BuildAuthRequestForSubmit()` builds the auth line items out of the **live POS basket** (engine-computed price, VAT, discount and the three deductible buckets), so the cost is reproducing what the till gave WPF for free. Dispensing/invoicing/GS1/robot ruled out of scope — the pharmacist keeps the till
