## The accountant's settlement account — spec 267 (tickets 268-274)

The frontend half of BackOffice map
[1142](file:///C:/Work/DMSCO/BackOffice/.issues/1142-store-settlement-account-map.md) / spec
[1173](file:///C:/Work/DMSCO/BackOffice/.issues/1173-store-settlement-account-spec.md), whose
Out-of-Scope hands this repo the accountant's screen and nothing above the endpoints. HQ posts
**SHORTAGE** (عجز) and **SURPLUS** (فائض) entries per store; a till consumes them at a shift close or
through a special receipt the branch prepares. **The endpoints have no caller — that is the whole
effort.** The design is already owner-ruled from a spike rebuilt at estate scale
(`C:\Work\DMSCO\BackOffice\.scratch\proto\settlement-accountant-screen\`, branch
`proto/1147-accountant-screen`; **`fake.js` is the read model**, and it is *not* this repo's code).
🔑 Two findings shape everything: **nobody browses 1394 branches**, so the door is a search box and a
triaged worklist with the branch account as the *destination*; and **an untriaged "needs you" list is
just another list** — the prototype proved it by burying four real problems under 131 ageing ones.

- [267](267-settlement-account-web-spec.md) — The accountant's settlement account — spec · **ready** · one feature in the existing collection area (`/collection/settlement`), gated as a **fifth grant** under the Collections access key — **no feature flag; the grant is the off-switch** · sliced into 268–274 · 🔑 scope defaults to **my branches** and widens freely, **except wrong-money and cash-waiting which stay estate-wide** (1255 of 1394 branches are unassigned and their money must not fall out of the door)
- [268](268-the-settlement-screen-appears-for-a-granted-session.md) — The Settlement screen appears only for a granted session · **done 2026-08-13** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: — · the feature, route, namespace, menu item and gate; lands as an empty shell. 🔑 Makes one decision for every ticket after it: **copy `ScreenGate` or graduate it to `@/core/ui`** — features never import features, and a third session inventing a third answer is the failure mode · **built**: 🔑 **GRADUATED**, and the evidence was already written down — 263 had made the **second** copy, and spec 261 §Out of Scope pre-registered the trigger (*"two copies is the duplication the rule accepts, and a **third** area is the trigger"*). Both copies **deleted**; `@/core/ui/ScreenGate` takes `query` (the **options**, not just the key — 257's ruling, since react-query merges concurrent observers and a dropped `retry:false` would retry a *refusal*), `can` and `ns` (the five `access.*` sentences differ per area — core owns the **shape**, not the copy), with `children` **required** on 263's stricter contract. ⚠️ **A second graduation the boundary forced**: `check-boundaries` reads `collection/inquiry` and `collection/settlement` as two features, so the ticket's "access call in the feature's own `api.ts`" was impossible — the **shared half** of the probe moved to `@/core/collection/api` (the 118/125/211 road), while 🚩 **each predicate stayed with its screen**, because 244 §10's independence is exactly what a core module holding all five would erode into a tier. ⚠️ **The screen is shut for every real session today and that is the design**: `canOpenSettlement` is a **required** fifth boolean the live door does not answer, so `=== true` denies and **no feature flag** was added to open it early (D1: the grant is the only off-switch) — which also means both Proof bullets are only observable against a stub, as 253's were. Proof: **1563** tests (23 new), `settlement-drive` **20/20** (new; 269–273 extend it), and because the gate touched five *shipped* screens, `collection-drive` **220/220** + `invoice-drive` **79/79** re-run as regression, unchanged
- [269](269-a-branchs-account-is-the-destination.md) — A branch's account opens, with its journal underneath · **done 2026-08-13** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: 268 · the **destination**, built before the door because a door onto nothing cannot be judged. Three rules that are the actual work: a consumption with **no document is named in words**, never a blank cell; **`REVERSE` rows render as restorations, not spends**; and **never difference confirmed against system cash across receipt kinds** (a settlement receipt carries `SystemCashTotal = 0`). Built against the fixture's **six hostile branches** before any happy path · **built**: the three rules are **structural, not stylistic** — rule 1 returns a tagged union (`{kind:'orphan'}`) so a formatter is handed a *case* and cannot render an empty cell; rule 2 is `isRestoration`, computed once so there is one place it can be wrong; rule 3 is enforced by **absence documented at the file someone would add it to**, the only subtraction in the feature being the headline's own, labelled *displayed, never consumed*. ⚠️ **D8 carries no currency**, so `Settlement/Account` gains a store header (`storeId`/`storeName`/`currencyKey`) — without it D10 is unimplementable and every Bahraini branch silently rounds; 274's to confirm, logged in `.afk/HITL-269.md` with the operator-id-not-name call. 🚩 **Two findings the fixture produced that the ticket did not name**: a `CANCELLED` entry keeps its **full `remainingAmount`** on the wire (0688/147 still carries 180.000), so the Remaining column now draws `—` where the headline already refused to count it — a grid and a headline saying different things about one entry is worse than either being wrong alone; and `writtenOff` is the journal's **own last `remainingAfter`** read back (400.000 on 0688/133), never a difference of two totals, which is the shape rule 3 forbids. `cap.ts` + `AccountStates.tsx` are **copies, not graduations** — 268's rubric applied: second copy, and *not* identical (the cap is **500**, the account door's `TOP`, against the inquiries' 2,000; only 3 of `GridStates`' 5 pieces came across, 269 being read-only). ⚠️ **`?store=` is the whole of how a branch is reached** (257's `?acr=` idiom) — the ticket forbids a picker, so 270 becomes a door onto an address rather than a rewiring. Two defects were caught **by eyeballing the six and by nothing else** (a journal column with no trailing gap reading as one token; a doubled `Z Z-40318`). Four `/code-review` findings fixed in-slice, two of them real: `agNumberColumnFilter` fed a **string** `filterValueGetter` (`equals 4` matches nothing, `lessThan 4` everything — confidently wrong, not merely unhelpful), and the journal fell back to `rows[0]` **with no row selected**, so after a sort it described an entry that was neither highlighted nor on top. Proof: **1596** tests (33 new, every assertion against a hostile branch), `settlement-drive` **51/51** (extended from 20, not replaced), 493 boundaries clean
- [270](270-the-door-searches-and-triages.md) — The door searches, and triages what needs a human · **done 2026-08-13** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: 269 · search by code / name in either script / city / entry number, plus three lanes triaged **by what they cost** — wrong money enumerated in full with a **Repair** action, cash waiting showing age, ageing collapsed to a count. 🔑 The estate-wide carve-out gets its own test, because it is the first thing a scope "tidy-up" breaks. A repair whose document arrived mid-click is a **no-op, not a failure** · **built**: the fixture is the other half of the deliverable — **1394 branches, 1255 unassigned, 24 mine**, 140 ageing of which **47** in scope, 4 orphans, seeded-LCG deterministic, with 269's six hostile branches computed *from their own accounts* so fleet and account cannot disagree, and 🔑 **0331 (the orphan) deliberately unassigned** so the carve-out is on screen and not only in a test. 🔑 **The carve-out is enforced three times by SHAPE**: the worklist door takes **no scope parameter**, `buildWorklist` hands the resolution to exactly one line, and `AgeingLane` carries a count and **no row array** — a card-per-entry is not a thing a component could do wrong. The fleet is fetched **once at `scope=all`** and scoped client-side; a scoped fetch would need a second estate-wide one beside it or would drop the 1255 from the lanes. ⚠️ **D8 could not carry the door**, so two doors were invented and logged: `Settlement/Worklist` (the aggregated `FleetRow` says a branch *has* an orphan; Repair is keyed by a consumption id) and `Settlement/Ledger` (*"entry 143, whichever branch"* — the flat view AND the search box), plus four `FleetRow` fields. 🚩 **Two ambiguities the fixture found**: `1001` is both a real branch code and a real entry number (493 collisions in the fixture) so an **exact code beats an entry number**, else an accountant typing their own code lands on another branch's account; and every link was throwing the scope away, so `addresses.ts` owns the URL grammar as a **keep-list** (a later slice's parameter belongs to a *view* by default). Open question **settled: manual refresh** + 60s `staleTime`. Six `/code-review` findings fixed in-slice, four real (uncaught `fetchQuery`; *"nothing needs a human"* rendering over a **failed** worklist door; the scope-losing back link). `/standards-review` clean on both axes; its three duplications graduated — `round3` → `@/core/money.roundMoney` at its **third** copy, the two report-figure folds → `figures.ts`, the two grids' shared cells → `entry-cells.ts`. Proof: **1659** tests (63 new), `settlement-drive` **88/88** (extended from 51), 511 files boundary-clean
- [271](271-one-entry-posts-and-reads-itself-back.md) — One entry posts, and the screen reads it back in words · **done 2026-08-13** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: 269 · **one form, one kind toggle** stating the consequence not the word; the branch is **typed and resolved to exactly one match**. 🔑 Two guards against two different mistakes: the amount **read back in words** (a review step, never a cap — approval limits are deliberately unsettled) and the branch's **standing open position of the same kind** shown before commit, since the duplicate is permitted by design and only the screen can catch it · **built**: one `PostEntryDialog` with an optional `seedStoreId`, opened from the **door** (branch typed) and from a **branch account** (branch seeded into the same typed box, resolving through the same one-match rule — a saved keystroke, not a bypass). 🔑 **The read-back is split where the i18n rule forces the split**: the number words are a pure, tested module (`amount-words.ts`), the currency's own nouns are the namespace's with `_one`/`_other` plurals — so *one riyal* is not *1 riyals* on the one screen where a **sentence** is the guard. ⚠️ **D4's rounding tension settled in writing**: the client rounds **nothing** on the way out (`50000.567` goes up verbatim, drive-asserted), the read-back is drawn at the **branch's own precision** (D10's existing rendering rule, not a second rounding rule), and the confirmation reads back the **server's** stored figure — with a line naming **both** figures if they ever differ, which is a path the drive's own stub cannot exercise and which is therefore written rather than proven. 🚩 **The standing-position guard needed no new door**: it filters the account answer this screen already fetches, on the same query key, so posting from an account costs no request — and it says *what it read* rather than *what the branch holds* when that answer failed or hit the door's 500-row `TOP`. Four `/code-review` findings fixed in-slice, all real: a pending or **failed** fleet made the form tell an accountant their own branch does not exist; `0.004` at a SAR branch parsed, posted as zero and read back as *zero riyals* (refused now — and that is the opposite end from the forbidden cap, which the drive proves by committing nine million unimpeded); the capped-account claim; and a confirmation destroyed by the refetch its own write triggered. `/standards-review` clean on hard rules, and its spec axis found the last one was **still** broken by a two-site mount (React remounts across the branch and the open-effect clears the confirmation) — the three states are now a **body**, not three returns, so the dialog has exactly one render site. `REASON_MAX` graduated to `posting.ts` at its second copy: two constants documented as *"the same 200"* and enforced in neither. Proof: **1688** tests (39 new), `settlement-drive` **117/117** (extended from 88), lint clean · ⚠️ `/domain-modeling` owes this wave a `CONTEXT.md` entry before 274 — *settlement entry / shortage / surplus*, and **branch**, which the glossary's Store entry currently lists under *Avoid*
- [272](272-one-button-corrects-and-the-audit-reads-as-time.md) — One button corrects an entry, and the audit reads as one column of time · **done 2026-08-14** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: 269 · the screen **never offers both acts** — untouched ⇒ Cancel, partly consumed ⇒ Write off the remainder. 🔑 A cancel that **loses the race** to a till must recover into the write-off carrying the true remaining, never an error toast (a refusal is a 200 with `accepted: false`). Audit borrows the authz pane's **shape and none of its storage** — that table is UTC, every settlement stamp is local · **built**: the one button is a **union, not two predicates** — there is deliberately no `canCancel`/`canWriteOff`, because two predicates a caller can ask independently are two a caller can ask **both** of, and the menu this ticket exists to keep at one item is back. 🔑 **The race recovery is the same rule over the server's newer truth**, not a patch on the old one (`afterRefusedCancel` re-runs `correctionFor` on the returned remaining), and it answers **three** outcomes because three things genuinely come back: the ticket's `partly-consumed`, plus `nothing-left` (a till took **all** of it mid-dialog — a write-off of zero is an act whose audit row says an accountant forgave nothing) and `refused` (the remaining did not move, so this is not the race). ⚠️ **Equality is at the scale money is HELD at**, not the branch's display precision: a BHD entry consumed by one fils is partly consumed, and rounding that away at 2 dp would offer *Cancel* on an entry the server refuses — manufacturing this ticket's own race. 🚩 **A panel above the journal, not a modal** — diverging from 270's and 271's dialogs on purpose, since *"the journal stays on screen, unchanged, under the act"* is load-bearing and a dialog covering it could not show it (drive asserts `dialog` count 0 mid-act, and 1 → 1 journal rows across the write-off). The audit pane takes the **projected row**, so the two panes cannot be handed different journals and the write-off's figure is 269's `writtenOff` read back rather than a second derivation — rule 3 holds, no subtraction in either module. ⚠️ **The write-off confirmation names NO figure**: D8 gives that answer one `remainingAmount`, and on success *what was forgiven* and *what is left* are both readings of it, differing by the whole amount. Three contract gaps transcribed rather than tidied, all for 274: no `refusalReason` on close-out, no closer **name** (staff id renders, as 269 did for the operator), and a **void and a repair are the same row** on this wire. Two defects caught **by eyeballing and nothing else**: the audit row's ` — ` separator sat *inside* the `dir="auto"` span, so an Arabic reason reordered the run and collided with the poster's name; and the write-off row stated no figure when *how much was forgiven* is what a reader came for. `/code-review` five findings (four here, all fixed) — a refused cancel **re-drew the identical Cancel button**, the forbidden press-refuse-press loop, and in the `nothing-left` case a live Cancel under *"there is nothing left to cancel"*; a race offer **permanently overrode fresh account data**. `/standards-review` clean on hard rules: the reason box was on its **third copy and had already drifted** (only the posting form's had `dir="auto"`, so one screen laid the same Arabic sentence out two ways) → `ReasonField`, extracted **within the feature** per that rule's ladder, with the four-key invalidation fan-out; the close-out's refusal was `-1`-in-a-component while the cancel's had a tested union → `afterRefusedCloseOut`; and its spec axis found the refusal handling **stranded** an accountant on a still-`OPEN` entry, so a dismiss button restores the affordance. Proof: **1730** tests (40 new), `settlement-drive` **148/148** (extended from 117), 523 files boundary-clean · ⚠️ `/domain-modeling` still owes this wave its `CONTEXT.md` entry — *branch* was kept unchanged from 268–271 rather than re-decided a fifth time in isolation
- [273](273-a-months-audit-uploads-and-commits.md) — A month's audit uploads, previews, and commits · **done 2026-08-14** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: 271 · the second door, ruled on the fact that **finance's audit already ends in a spreadsheet**. Two calls over one upload — **commit re-sends the file**, no staging table, hash mismatch refused. 🔑 271's guard **does not survive multiplication**: a **preview grid resolving every code to a branch name** (the only guard that catches the right amount on the wrong branch) plus **the file total in words**. Hard errors all-or-nothing; duplicate warnings commit; **one kind per file**, or the total is a net figure a typo hides inside · **built**: `@/core/api` grew the multipart door (`api.upload` — `post` with a `FormData` body, and 🔑 **no Content-Type at all**, so the browser's own `boundary=` survives; a hand-set JSON one is a failure that looks like an empty file). 🔑 **Both guards, at both levels**: the preview grid resolves every code to a branch name — and an unnamed row is a hard error *even if the server reported none*, the client's own backstop — while the file's total rides **in words ON the commit button**, folded per currency from the previewed rows, with D8's scalar `total` demoted to a cross-check because it cannot describe a mixed file. 271's guard **lifted, not multiplied**: no per-row read-back. ⚠️ **Cancel-as-a-unit is a loop over 272's own `Settlement/Cancel`** and the batch is an **address** (`?view=batch&batch=`), reachable an hour and a reload later because the `batchId` became a criterion of 270's ledger (one input, one column) rather than a *list my batches* door nobody asked for; the withdrawal reports three groups and rolls nothing back — withdrawn, the rows a till got to first (**named**, with the server's words and the new remaining), and the calls that never completed, whose entries are *unknown* rather than decided. Reviews found seven, all fixed — the two that mattered: a `catch` that reported a **business refusal as an unknown** (`api-envelope`), and a commit fallback that **asserted the hash** on every failure, including ones this client cannot know about (269's cap banner, again). Proof: **1760** tests (23 new), `settlement-drive` **177/177**
- [274](274-the-screen-calls-the-real-door.md) — The screen calls the real door · **done 2026-08-15** · *(spec 267)* · blocked by: 270, 272, 273 · ⚠ **excluded from any AFK run**. BackOffice's side is now **built** — 1173's tickets 1174–1187 are all done on `pricing2`, migration 081 applied, and 1185/1186 shipped the six doors, the orphan lane and the three bulk doors. 🔑 **The routes and casing were RIGHT; the shapes were not — and the shapes were mostly ours.** D13 specifies six doors and bulk; every 'missing' field was added client-side across 269–273 as a D8 extension and never negotiated, so this ticket settled them by **removing them** and narrowed the screen to D13. 🚩 **Worst find: the fleet door's default `TOP` is 500 and the estate is 1394** — `fleet()` named no `limit`, so the front page would have drawn 894 branches missing as *the estate*, with nothing in the answer to say so. Also fixed: `Settlement/Worklist` does not exist (→ `Settlement/Orphans`); the repair answer's `remainingAfter` is **`remainingAmount`** (read `undefined`); close-out DOES carry `refusalReason`+`status`, closing HITL-272's gap; the **client** mints the `batchId` and it must be a **ULID** (entry ids are derived from it, so a near-collision silently replays); commit must echo `contentHash` and **a hash mismatch is a 200 with `accepted:false`**, not an `ApiError`; and ✅ **`Settlement/Bulk/Cancel` exists**, replacing ~150 lines of client loop that had stood on a ledger door that does not exist. **Removed, not stubbed**: the cross-estate ledger, the cash-waiting and ageing lanes, `assignment` ranking, `city` search, the entry-number lookup. ⚠️ **The money consequence**: no read door carries `currencyKey`, so figures render min-2/max-3 decimals and are worded at the ledger's own three — wording a Bahraini `95.505` at two would read back *95.51*, get approved, and store `95.505`, which is what D4 exists to prevent. 🚩 New guard: `worklist.test.ts` asserts the **fixture emits exactly D13's fields** — a fixture richer than the wire is why this went unseen for five tickets. Hand-off drafted at `.afk/BACKOFFICE-TICKET-DRAFT-settlement-reads.md`, asking for three things only: `currencyKey` on the reads, `Settlement/Uncollected`, `Settlement/Ledger`. ✅ **The live pass then ran, and the grant never had to be seeded**: `CollectionScreenGate` is a plain engine `Check`, so the `*/*` wildcard resolves `[SettlementAccount,03]` with no settlement row in existence — ⚠️ through account **`14419`**, *not* `ADMIN`, which holds the wildcard but is a **shared** account web sign-in refuses by design (`WEBAUTH-90030`). Both directions proven, **no permanent grant holder left behind**. 🔑 **THE FLEET AGGREGATE IS FAST**: with **1394 open entries across all 1394 branches** (seeded through the real bulk door, withdrawn again), `Settlement/Fleet?scope=all&limit=2000` answers in **38–76 ms** for 295 KB — so the design's no-cache, no-denormalised-balance ruling **holds**, and the read-modify-write trap it refused twice was refused correctly; the bulk *writes* are the slow calls (commit 9.4 s, batch-cancel 12.4 s at 1394 rows, ~7–9 ms/row). Driven live, wire **and** screen: a real entry posted (`123.456` → **123**, D15's whole-unit SAR rounding; `0.3` → **400 `SettlementAmountRoundsToZero`**), cancelled, and a re-cancel answering **200 / `accepted:false` / `ENTRY_NOT_OPEN`**; a remainder **written off through the correction pane** against a real till consumption of 26.00 of 35.00 on a `SHIFT_CLOSE`; a real `.xlsx` previewed with resolved Arabic names and both warning codes, committed, and the batch **withdrawn as a unit**; a sheet **edited between preview and commit** refused **200 / `HASH_MISMATCH`** — A6 confirmed and 273's reading refuted by the server itself; and the batch ids proven ULID-only twice, the entry ids arriving as `B` + ordinal + `batchId[5..]`, which is *why*. 🚩 **The picker defect proven gone on the screen**: the post form resolved branch **1257** — *served by nobody, carrying nothing* — and posted onto it, which the fleet-backed picker could never have found. ⚠️ **Two things this sink could not settle, recorded not faked**: the orphan lane is legitimately empty (an orphan needs `DocumentId=''` past the 72-hour `const` grace) so **`Settlement/Repair` was never exercised**; and scoping/the carve-out cannot be judged while **zero** of 1394 assignment rows have both slots filled. 🚩 §B6 narrowed to one door — **`currencyKey` on `Settlement/Branches`** — because the post form resolves its branch there *before* an amount is typed, so `512.75` was worded at three decimals and corrected to **513** only after the post returned. **The live pass changed no client code**, which is the finding: the contract work was right. Proof: typecheck + lint + build green, **1828** tests, `settlement-drive` **189/189**; full write-up `.afk/FINDINGS-274.md` §C. ✅ **Two doors were then BUILT server-side, the owner lifting this ticket's read-only boundary twice** (BackOffice draft 1199 §5 and §3), each proven by an end-to-end test against the live database and each left **uncommitted** in that repo. `Settlement/Branches` — the open `Store` master — because the picker resolved a typed branch against the **fleet**, which is *branches with settlement activity*, so on a migrated-but-unused database no first entry could be posted at all; 🔑 the collection-assignment master **ranks and labels** that list (`servedBy`, session-resolved `isMine`) and deliberately does **not** gate it — 1255 of 1394 branches are paired to nobody, so a filter would make their shortages unpostable by anyone while the bulk lane kept reaching them. `Settlement/Ledger` — entries across the estate — because 1173 mints `entryNumber`, calls it *the handle finance and the branch settle by on the phone*, and leaves no door that resolves one (`Settlement/Account` takes the `storeId` you are ringing up to ask for); it also restores the answer to **"what is still owed out there"**, which the triaged front page deliberately does not give. 🚩 It **refuses an unfiltered call** (`SettlementLedgerCriterionRequired`) — a cap is honest when it truncates an answer, not when it makes one — and its rows carry `currencyKey`, so nothing totals a column holding riyals and dinars. ⚠️ Three asks remain for 1199: `currencyKey` on the account and fleet reads, `Settlement/Uncollected`, and 🔑 **the grant seed — still the deploy blocker**

## Settlement follow-up lanes — map 275 (tickets 276-281) → spec 282 → build 283-288

The two lanes ticket [274](274-the-screen-calls-the-real-door.md) **removed rather than faked** when
it pointed the settlement screen at a live SIS.Api: **entry ageing** (`.afk/FINDINGS-274.md` §B3) and
**cash waiting / uncollected receipts** (§B2). 274's refusal was right — the ageing lane needed a
staleness threshold BackOffice spec 1173 declines to mint (*"whether an entry goes stale is fog"*) —
but it left an accountant with no way to chase a branch that has not sent the money. 🔑 **The
charting ruling that unblocks it: state age as a FACT and never judge it**, so no threshold is
needed and 1173's ruling stands untouched. Driver: today the only tool is a hand-typed ledger query
carrying no ownership, no age sort and no record of who was already rung.

- [275](275-settlement-follow-up-lanes-map.md) — Settlement follow-up lanes: entry ageing + cash waiting · **map, open — ✅ route complete 2026-08-15, all six tickets 276–281 resolved; next step `/to-spec`** · dest = a `ready` spec for one follow-up screen at `/collection/settlement/open`, tabs **Owing · Owed · Cash waiting** · 🔑 no threshold (age as fact) · estate-wide **ranked mine-first** via `ServedBy`/`IsMine`, the carve-out 267 holds for wrong money extended to ageing (1255 of 1394 branches are assigned to nobody) · SHORTAGE and SURPLUS **visibly split** — same age fact, opposite direction · a **minimal append-only chase note** in scope, its BackOffice dependency isolated so the read-only lanes ship regardless · the nav grows a **third level** and the views become **paths** · out of scope: workflow statuses, a per-branch cadence table, any notification rail

## The web creates a bonded return — spec 289 (frontend half of BackOffice map 1266)

- [289](289-bonded-return-screen-spec.md) — The screen that creates a bonded return · **spec, ready** · *(frontend half; the BACKEND spec [1283](file:///C:/Work/DMSCO/BackOffice/.issues/1283-web-bonded-return-door-spec.md) in the sibling BackOffice repo is the **normative owner of every server shape** — §2/§2b are transcribed here under one provenance line)* · fills in the `return-document` placeholder on Document Details as a **dialog**, not a route — a return is one decision about the delivery you are looking at, and `ChangeStoreDialog` already proves a grid in a modal is the house pattern · arrangement is not designed here: it was built, driven in a browser and approved as [1270's HTML build target](file:///C:/Work/DMSCO/BackOffice/.issues/assets/1270-return-screen-build-target.html) · 🔑 **no `BZ02` and no `BB` anywhere in React** — `commands.ts`'s `isBeyondBorder` gate is *wrong* (it enables at SMSA stores, where a return persists, books no pickup and never refunds, silently) and is replaced by the server's `canReturn`, rendered **disabled-with-a-reason** in three distinguishable cases · ⚠ one flag carries two of the three causes, so the screen splits *store* from *exhausted* off its own line projection — a **reason-string** choice only, `disabled` follows `canReturn` alone · one pure `return-order.ts` owns the line projection (fully-returned lines **hidden** and counted), the fee projection (item-0 `condAmount`, never `condValue`, never summed with the `'H'` copies), the one-complaint-at-a-time submit gate, the `[1, remaining]` clamp, and the **request builder** — whose whole-body test asserts **no key carries an amount** · `requestId` minted per dialog *opening*, not per submit, so a retry replays onto the same return; a replay renders as **plain success** · refusal is a toast **plus a banner that stays**, selections intact · address is a collapsed summary, **absent — not disabled — under `RF`**, district picker reusing the cached `SdDocument/Districts` lookup · no box count, no total weight, **no grand total** · seams: pure vitest + a new `tools/return-dialog-drive.mjs`, **no RTL this wave** (owner-confirmed); four checked-in fixtures, no msw — shapes contractual, **values are not** · ⚠ 1283 §2b never names the returned-quantity field, so `returnedQuantity` here is a **guess**, and 🔑 **the joining ticket IS the drift detector**: a 400 there is a drift report against 1283 §2/§2b and *the spec is corrected first, never the transcription alone*

## IDoc Inspector screen (BackOffice spec 1386 / map 1374)

Client half of the read-only SAP IDoc viewer. A consultant enters store + TrxNumber and sees every IDoc
the NEW rail generated — documents, lines, conditions, item details, payments, FI — with provenance,
plus an XML download. Server half is BackOffice `.issues/1387–1394`; each ticket below names its `dep:`.
Lands under `features/reports/idoc-inspector/`, beside `retail-invoice`. **No client flag — the authz
grant is the switch.**

- [296](296-idoc-inspector-screen-access-spine.md) — theIDocInspectorScreenGatesOnItsOwnGrant · **done** · blocked by: — · dep: BackOffice 1387 (grant seed + gate + `Access`) · Feature scaffold in the `reports` area, own i18n namespace (zero-literal from the first commit), nav + in-page guard off the access probe. 🔑 **Denied is a 200, not a failure** — render a shut door, never a retry, or the user is told to try again forever · **done 2026-08-30**: feature under `reports` beside `retail-invoice`, route `/reports/idoc-inspector`, nav leaf and in-page `ScreenGate` on ONE probe key (`IDocInspector/Access`), a two-field lookup bar and a landing state. 🔑 **Denied renders as a shut door**: `retry: false` + `staleTime: Infinity` travel WITH the key (react-query merges concurrent observers' options, so a reader that dropped `retry` would retry a permanent no), and the drive proves the sentence names an administrator and never a retry. 🔑 **Its own grant, its own key** — `menu-reports.test.ts` now answers the group's two probes SEPARATELY and asserts both mixed cases, so a menu that gated invoices and the inspector together fails. ⚠ **i18n is the AREA's namespace** (`reports`, keys under `idocInspector.*`) per `core/i18n.ts`, NOT the per-feature namespace the ticket's own text asks for — logged in `.afk/HITL-296.md`. That forced one core change: `ScreenGate` grew an optional `keyPrefix` (default `'access'`, every prior caller byte-identical), because one area namespace now holds two screens' denial sentences and a consultant refused here must not be told they lack the grant for *invoices*; the invoices copy moved to `invoice.access.*` and the top-level block was **deleted**, so a third screen that forgets the prop renders a raw key (loud) rather than the wrong screen's sentence. ⚠ **The door is UNBUILT** (BackOffice 1387 open) — so the probe deliberately does NOT degrade to *allowed* the way `Bby/Access` did, and the drive stubs the envelopes: **nothing here has met a live SIS.Api**. `sameLookup` was written and then REMOVED — the re-ask rule belongs to the slice that first hangs a query off the key (297), not to a helper with no caller. Glossary gained **IDoc**, **IDoc batch** (⚠ vs batch/CHARG), **parked entry**, **verdict**, **source tag** · reviews: `/code-review` high caught three (a broken sibling nav test, two drive checks the landing hint satisfied on its own, and an `aria-describedby` that announced the wrong field's error) — all fixed; `/standards-review` found **no hard rule violation and no spec defect**, and its one deferred call is graduating the copied access-probe shape to `core/` (third instance), left for the owner and logged · drive **29/29** + invoice **79/79**, 2006 pure cases, typecheck + lint + build green
